# Spec 2 W6 (Dating chat) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Summary (one paragraph).** W6 is three batches, ten tasks, three migrations (each with its grants twin), one new edge function, and the dating chat on the app. **W6a** (Tasks 1–4) → `20261120100000_dating_v2_chat.sql` (+ `20261120100010_dating_v2_chat_grants.sql`): one contact masker in SQL (`private.dating_mask_contacts`, with its line-by-line TypeScript mirror and a 207-case parity fixture) that masks the card's captions instead of dropping them, the one-way pair marker the owner asked for (written on Not for me, Block, Unmatch and Close kindly, read by generation's never-introduced-before filter, kept 2 years), carry 5's held identity so a move can't keep a man–woman pair open, act naming the match it made (Task 1); the chat itself: `dating_messages`, `dating_chat_signals`, send with its fixed refusals, the paged read, the conversations read, swap from 72 h, Block and Unmatch with fixed answers, the dragnet's first-message input and the open-chats count from real messages, the two race cases (Task 2); the app's data layer (Task 3); the conversations list, the chat screen, its sheets, "Say hello", and the W6a gate (Task 4). **W6b** (Tasks 5–7) → `20261120110000_dating_v2_chat_safety.sql` (+ `…110010_…_grants.sql`): the chat's own outbox, the versioned lexicon config (`dating_chat_v1`, frozen once active, `DEV-` refused in production), safety events for the recipient only, moderation cases and the three cross-message signals, the cards read, collapsed neutral pushes, the worker's crons (Task 5); the deterministic engine `_shared/dating_chat_safety.ts` on Spec 1's normaliser and the `process-dating-safety-outbox` worker (Task 6); the recipient's cards in the chat and the W6b gate (Task 7). **W6c** (Tasks 8–10) → `20261120120000_dating_v2_chat_guided.sql` (+ `…120010_…_grants.sql`): the guided first conversation, the day-7 and day-21 cards, We met, Close kindly, the check-in push (`me_checkin`, exempt from quiet hours, R17) and the daily retention purge (Task 8); the guided card, the timebox cards, Close kindly and We met in the chat (Task 9); the meeting kit, the check-in screen, the `me_checkin` push case behind the Dating lock, the chat safety spec v2.0, the manual, and the W6c gate (Task 10).

**Replayed.** Every task below was replayed in order in a scratch copy of the repo (on a clone of `attune_spec3a`): every "Create … with exactly" block, every build, edit and splice script, ARB fragment, `gen-l10n`, `dart format` and test command, every mutant set, the race script on an idle scratch database and the Deno CI line. The Expected lines are what that replay printed (Replay record, end of plan).

**Goal:** Build Spec 2's dating chat (§3.6, §6.1, W6) on the W1–W5 server and app: text-only messages between two matched people that never touch couples chat, contact details masked as "•••" until both tap Swap (offered from 72 hours), Block and Unmatch that hold against a stale client, a deterministic safety pipeline that never delays delivery and shows support only to the person who received the message, the guided first conversation, the day-7 and day-21 cards, the meeting kit with Check on me and Get help, Close kindly, a 30-day evidence window and the retention purge; and Dating still off in production.

**Architecture:** Server first in each batch, then the edge function, then the app. Every re-created SQL function is taken from its LIVE body (`pg_get_functiondef` on `attune_spec3a` after the earlier batches, saved to `$D/before_w6{a,b,c}/`) with only the lines marked `-- Spec 2 W6a` / `W6b` / `W6c` changed (`marked_diff.py` checks); the two whose return type changes (`block_dating_match`, `unmatch_dating_match`: `void` and a raise before, a fixed jsonb answer now, carry 4) are dropped and created whole, and say so. Masking is a presentation rule the server applies when it reads (§4.1 of the chat spec v2): the stored body is never changed, the masked copy is computed once at insert, and the swap state decides which one a read returns. Safety runs after delivery through the chat's own outbox and worker (fail-open): the engine is pure TypeScript on Spec 1's normaliser and a frozen, versioned config; the worker writes rule ids, never text. The app keeps every chat read in account-keyed `autoDispose` providers and route-scoped state (D8), refreshes on the per-person `dating_chat_signals` row (realtime, one row per person, no message content), holds the meet plan in memory only (D20), and sits behind the Dating lock gate (`datingRoute()`).

**Tech Stack:** Supabase Postgres 17 (RLS, `SECURITY DEFINER`, pg_cron, `extensions.hmac`, `extensions.digest`, realtime publication), psql, Python 3 (build, edit, splice and mutation scripts), Deno 2.8.1 (engine, worker and tests, frozen lock `.github/deno-w9.lock`), Flutter 3.29.1 / Dart 3.7 / Riverpod 2.6 / go_router, `supabase_flutter`, `share_plus` 7 and `url_launcher` (already dependencies). No new package.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-dating-mode-design.md` — §8 W6 (W6a, W6b, W6c, the gate line); §3.5 (Conversations list), §3.6 (the whole chat section), §3.13 (message pushes, the check-in), §3.15 (Chat, Meeting kit rows); §4.3 (`dating_messages`, `dating_message_safety_outbox`, `dating_safety_configs`, `dating_safety_events`, `dating_moderation_cases` signal kinds), §4.4, §4.5 (RLS), §4.6 (the worker, stale-worker and purge crons), §4.7 (`process-dating-safety-outbox`), §4.8 (retention), §4.9 W6a–W6c, §4.10 (every row tagged W6); §5.3 (open pre-date chats), §5.10 (the dragnet's first messages; duplicate opener, money asks, sexual pressure); §6.1 (masking, the lexicon, the cards' copy), §6.9 (metrics); §7.1 (`dating_v2_chat_contracts.sql`), §7.3 (masking parity, the worker), §7.4, §7.5 mutants 10, 11, 12, §7.6 (the chat worker and masking rows), §7.7 (W6's device checks); §1.4–§1.5 (the chat safety spec amendments, Q15, Q17, Q20). `lib/architecture/DATING_CHAT_SAFETY_SPEC.md` (v1 → v2.0 in Task 10). `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-cross-spec-review.md` — I-9, I-17, S-5, W-2. Carries: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-20-spec2-w6/carries.md` (all 13, mapped below; owner decision 1: the pair marker, in W6a). Format reference: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-19-spec2-w5-matching.md`.

## Global Constraints

- **Where and how.** Work in the main working tree on `main` (`/Users/user/attune`). HEAD is `b8340ae5` (S2 W1–W5 committed). The owner may have uncommitted edits (`supabase/.temp/cli-latest`, `backup-before-planning-p2.sql`): never revert, stash, stage, checkout or reformat them. Every edit to an existing file is an exact replacement made by a script that asserts each anchor occurs once (`anchor.py`); if an anchor moved because a later fix changed it, apply the same change to the current text and say so in the task report.
- **NO commits.** No `git add`, `git commit`, `git stash`, `git checkout`, `git reset`, `git rm` (delete files with `rm`). Where a skill says "commit", instead: **do not commit; record the tree in the report** with `T=$(mktemp -u); GIT_INDEX_FILE=$T git read-tree HEAD && GIT_INDEX_FILE=$T git add -A && GIT_INDEX_FILE=$T git write-tree; rm -f $T` (a private index; the real index is untouched). The controller commits after each batch's final review.
- **Formatting.** Never `dart format` a pre-existing file (the repo uses an older formatter; formatting reflows it). `dart format` only the files a task creates. A later task that edits a file an earlier W6 task created does it with a generated anchor script already in `dart format` style (`edit_app_T7.py`, `edit_app_T9.py`, `edit_app_T10.py`) and does not run the formatter on it.
- **SQL target.** Contracts run only on `attune_spec3a`, from the repo root. A file with a top-level `BEGIN;` runs as `psql -X -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -d attune_spec3a -f F`; one without runs wrapped (`suite.sh` does both). Migrations and their twins are applied with `psql -X -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -d attune_spec3a -f <file>`. Confirm the target first (`psql -X -d attune_spec3a -Atc 'select current_database()'` prints `attune_spec3a`). **NEVER `attune_test`. NEVER run `scripts/local_pg_setup.sh` or `scripts/local_pg_grants.sql`** — but DO append each new twin's `\i` line to `scripts/local_pg_grants.sql` (`grants_line.py`). Mutants run inside one transaction that the contract's own `ROLLBACK` ends (`mutate.py`, with the grants twin applied after each migration mutant). An `IF a OR b` in a DO block has no evaluation order: every side-effecting call is its own statement first. `CASE` inside `IF` needs parentheses. `INSERT … RETURNING` needs SELECT on the table: a contract that inserts as a signed-in person wraps it in a CTE that returns a constant.
- **Migrations.** `20261120100000_dating_v2_chat.sql`, `20261120110000_dating_v2_chat_safety.sql`, `20261120120000_dating_v2_chat_guided.sql`, each with its `…10_…_grants.sql` twin, later than every file in `supabase/migrations/` (the newest is `20261119120010_dating_v2_signals_grants.sql`). If `ls supabase/migrations | sort | tail -1` shows anything at or after `20261120100000` when Task 1 starts, shift all six to the next free hours keeping their order and change every header, `\i` line and command. Each file is one `BEGIN; … COMMIT;`, idempotent at its own task (applied twice, `exit=0` both times), with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'`. Every new function: `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = ''` (the pure ones `IMMUTABLE`), fully-qualified names, `REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC, anon` and `service_role` unless the spec gives it the service role (the worker's four: `get_dating_safety_config`, `claim_dating_safety_jobs`, `complete_dating_safety_job`, `recover_stale_dating_safety_jobs`; the purge), `GRANT EXECUTE … TO authenticated` only for the client RPCs; every grant is re-asserted in the twin. Re-created functions start from their LIVE body and change only lines marked `-- Spec 2 W6a|W6b|W6c` (`marked_diff.py` checks). Pre-validate input so no raw constraint error echoes input; answers are fixed `{ok, reason}` codes, never a raise but the unsigned caller; NULL never counts as eligible, current, swapped or safe. Shared lock order: the per-person setup lock (`private.dating_lock_setup`) first, then the match row, then its introduction row, with a bounded 40P01/55P03/40001 retry (3) then `busy`. Reads that write (the seen mark, the phone's last use) are `VOLATILE` (carry 11: PostgREST runs `STABLE` reads READ ONLY and the write is lost). Exposure-reducing writes (Block, Unmatch, Close kindly, a dismissed card) are never gated by the phone or eligibility; deletion and the purge never wait on generation; no cron step holds a lock across a loop; each purge step is its own error block counted by SQLSTATE; every new counter is a `dating.` metric (W1's `metric_key_prefixes`) and shows in health.
- **Separation (§4.3, chat spec v2 §1.4).** Dating messages never enter `public.messages`, `relationship_message_counters` or the couples safety outbox; the dating chat imports nothing from `lib/features/chat/**` or `lib/features/games/**` (`dating_boundaries_test.dart`). No LLM anywhere. Delivery never waits on safety (fail-open). Masking never lifts by itself: only both sides' swap marks lift it, and nothing ever clears a swap once both have swapped.
- **Production stays off.** No Dating flag turns on in production; no migration turns a flag on, activates a config or sets `attune_environment`. `supabase/sql/dev_enable_spec2_dating_flags.sql` turns every Dating flag ON in development and, from W6b, activates `dating_chat_v1` with `DEV-` review references; in production a trigger refuses to activate a config whose references start `DEV-`.
- **Vendors and data.** No vendor call in any test (Deno tests inject the RPC; no `--allow-net`). Never read `.env*`, credentials or the keychain. Safety events, cases, pushes, metrics and logs never hold message text; a push names no person and no match; the pair marker holds no name and no id (a keyed hash of the pair's two phone HMACs).
- **Copy.** New strings go into `lib/i10n/app_en.arb` under `dating_` only through `python3 -I .superpowers/sdd/2026-10-26-spec1-w3/arb_append.py <fragment>`, then `flutter gen-l10n --arb-dir=lib/i10n --template-arb-file=app_en.arb --output-dir=lib/i10n/generated --output-localization-file=app_localizations.dart --no-synthetic-package`. Straight apostrophes. Copy names people (a person with no name is "this person"), never "they" for the other person. Every displayed date or time goes through `MyDateFormat`. Exact spec strings are copied verbatim; strings this plan adds are marked "Plan copy" in their ARB description and listed for the reviewers (Owner checklist). The app never shows a server's text: every answer is a fixed code mapped to ARB copy.
- **UI.** Every Dating screen and sheet is under `AllowLargeText`; sheets carry `datingSheetSettings(...)` and `DatingSheetTracker`, and close when their screen is no longer on top. Error red is `#C62828` in light (`FlowErrorLine`) and the theme's own colour in dark; errors are icon-plus-text live regions; busy, offline and device answers are calm lines (`DatingCalmLine`, `DatingReverifyLine`). Goldens at 1.0, 1.3, 2.0 and RTL (`expectFlowGoldens`), the three guidelines (`expectFlowGuidelines`), a dark-theme render, the large-text audit at 2.0 (`0 site(s) to fix`). Taps in tests use `tapInReach` then pump. A `Semantics(excludeSemantics: true)` that is tappable carries `onTap`; a row of actions is a `container: true` node. Motion: the chat slides in (shared axis), messages never animate, safety cards slide in at 150 ms, nothing moves under reduce motion. Text fields never let the keyboard learn (`enableSuggestions`, `autocorrect`, `enableIMEPersonalizedLearning` all false).
- **D8 shared phones, D20.** Every Dating read is keyed to the signed-in account and every screen clears its state on an account change (the lock gate also rebuilds each Dating page per account, Ruling R-35); D8 tests first assert the page is on screen, then that nothing of the last account shows; a typed message is never sent as the next account. Nothing Dating is written to prefs, files, the image disk cache or secure storage; the meet plan lives in `DatingPlanMemory` only and is cleared on quick exit and sign-out through Dating's entry in Spec 1's device wipe registry.
- **Every answer settles.** One idempotency key per action (`DatingActionKeys`), settled once the answer is final; a busy or failed answer keeps it, so a retry is the same request.
- **Off limits.** In-chat Games (`test/architecture/games_untouched_test.dart` stays green), Opinions, `ATTUNE_SOUL.md`, `ATTUNE_THESIS.md`, `lib/home/home_screen.dart` and its latch (byte-identical; `shasum -c .superpowers/sdd/2026-11-07-spec2-w3/latch.sha`), `quick_exit_service.dart`, `auth_provider.dart`, `lib/main.dart`, `lib/app/app.dart`, `lib/app/routing/app_router.dart`, every applied migration, every Spec 1 file except the two the spec names (`notification_config.dart`'s `me_checkin` case; `scheduled_notifications_quiet_guard` re-created with `me_checkin`), every edge function but `process-dating-safety-outbox` (new), `_shared/unicode_normaliser.ts` and the couples `chat_safety.ts` (read, never edited). **No deploys** (`supabase db push`, function deploys, secrets and app builds are the owner's; Owner checklist).
- **Fixture ids.** The chat contract's people are `6c600000-0000-4000-8000-0000000000nn` with phones `+1555960nnnn`-style keyed on `nn`; ranges are reserved per batch so later sections never collide: A–E `01–29`, F–L `30–50`, M–Q `60–70`, R–W `72–84`. The race script's W6 cases use `41–44` in its own id space.
- **Gates, every batch (Tasks 4, 7 and 10).** (1) The wrapped SQL suite on `attune_spec3a`: no new FAIL (baseline **160 PASS / 14 FAIL**, or 159 / 15 when `quiet_hours` runs at its clock-dependent hour; **161 / 14** after Task 1 adds `dating_v2_chat_contracts.sql`). (2) Full `flutter test`: no failure beyond the known flakes outside Dating (`chat_controller_test:259`, `chat_screen_list_anchor` D<0, `reflection_journal` at midnight, `planning_recent`, `story_outbox_notices_test` "Discard asks first…", `chat_upload_progress_cancel_test`, `story_review_test` "final review #1"; re-run a failing file alone before calling it a flake); baseline **+8364**; the count moves by the tests each task adds (listed per task). (3) `flutter analyze --no-pub lib test`: **571 issues, 0 new**. (4) `shasum -c .superpowers/sdd/2026-11-04-spec1-w10-accessibility-docs/w10-1.0.sha` and `shasum -c .superpowers/sdd/2026-11-04-spec1-w9/flag_off.sha`: every line `OK`. (5) `scripts/concurrency/dating_kill_switch_races.sh` on an IDLE scratch DB built from `pg_dump -s attune_spec3a` plus the config tables' data (`public.metric_key_prefixes`, `public.feature_flags`, `public.country_safety_config`, `private.dating_calling_codes`, `public.dating_algorithm_configs`, and from W6b `public.dating_safety_configs`), dropped after: every case `PASS` (37 from W6a on). (6) The Deno CI line (`.github/workflows/tests.yml` job `deno-w9`, frozen lock) passes in full (209 before W6, 214 after W6a, 225 after W6b) and its `deno check` line is clean. (7) `test/architecture/games_untouched_test.dart` passes and the latch is `OK`. (8) Each task's named mutants all RED (a `CONTRACT VIOLATED` line or `KILLED`), the RED line quoted in the report; a `COMPILE` or an `anchor missing` is a broken mutant to fix, never a kill; an equivalent mutant is retired with its reason in the task, never silently.


## Review Focus

Five things a real person will meet that this wave must survive. Each is pinned to tests that fail if it regresses.

1. **A message sent after a Block or an Unmatch, from a stale app** (the other person blocked a second ago; the sender's screen still shows the chat, or a modified client calls the RPC or inserts directly). Expect: nothing is stored or delivered after the block commits, the sender sees "This conversation has ended." and the person who blocked is never notified; a Block and the other's Leave at the same moment finish without a deadlock. Tests: Task 2 contract H (with the chat still open, the next send after a block by account, a block by phone HMAC alone and an unmatch answers `closed` and stores nothing; no API role can insert a row, and the INSERT policy refuses the blocked pair; mutants `m12_send_skips_block`, `can_send_ignores_hmac`, `policy_open`, `block_chat_untold`); Task 2 race cases `a send while the other blocks: nothing after the block` and `the chat's Block while the other leaves: no deadlock`; Task 4 widget `a send shows Sending…, then nothing; a failure says so and a tap tries the same request again`.
2. **A phone number, handle or link before both people swap**, written to slip past a filter (spaced or dotted digits, number words in English or Twi, leetspeak, look-alike Unicode letters, `wa.me`, "the gram", an email with " at "), in a message or a photo caption, or when only one side has tapped Swap. Expect: "•••" in every read the other person can reach (the chat, the conversations list's last line, the card's caption), the sender's own copy unmasked, a caption that invites contact dropped, nothing unmasked by one side's swap or before 72 hours, and once both swap nothing is ever masked again. Tests: Task 1 contract A (all 207 fixture cases through the SQL masker) and B (captions; mutants `mask_no_phones`, `mask_stop_ignored`, `mask_safe_raw`, `caption_raw`, `caption_invite_shown`); Task 1 Deno `every case masks to its expected output` (the TypeScript mirror, same fixture); Task 2 contract I (mutants 10 and 11: `m10_one_side_unmasks`, `m11_swap_early`; `send_unmasked`, `read_raw`); Task 4 widgets (`chat_masked_read_raw`, `list_masked_read_raw`, `swap_open_early`).
3. **A pair that passed, blocked, unmatched or closed kindly, introduced again** after one of them leaves Dating and returns, or makes a new account on the same number. Expect: never introduced again, by any generation run, for two years; the marker holds no name and no id. Tests: Task 1 contract C (Not for me, Block and generation's filter 14 read the marker after both leave and return; a new account on the same number; mutants `marker_pass_unwritten`, `marker_block_unwritten`, `marker_predicate_ignored`, `marker_generation_ignored`, `marker_keyed_to_accounts`, `marker_one_year`); Task 2 contract H (`unmatch_no_marker`); Task 8 contract T (`close_kindly_no_marker`) and V (the purge keeps markers 2 years; `markers_kept_forever`).
4. **The safety pipeline fails** (no active config, a malformed config, an engine exception, a worker that dies mid-batch). Expect: the message was already delivered and stays delivered; nothing is shown to anyone by mistake; the job retries, dead-letters after 5 attempts, and every failure is a content-free counter in health; a failed config never falls back to the couples rules. Tests: Task 5 contract M (`outbox_not_queued`) and N (claim, complete with an error, dead letter at 5, stale recovery, no config; mutants `never_dead_letters`, `claim_for_clients`); Task 6 Deno `no config, a malformed one or one that fails to load: no detection`, `one message's failure is its own; an engine error goes back with a code`, `nothing logged holds a message's text` (mutants `no_config_named`, `engine_error_lost`, `log_holds_text`).
5. **An account switch on a shared phone while a chat or a meet plan is open** (a sibling signs in on the same phone mid-conversation, with a message typed or a plan's place filled in). Expect: no frame of the next account shows the last account's messages, names, cards or plan; the typed message is never sent as the next account; quick exit and sign-out forget the plan. Tests: Task 3 `D8: the list is one entry per account; the next account's starts loading and holds nothing of the last`; Task 4 widget `D8: another account sees nothing of this chat, and nothing typed is sent as it` (asserts the chat page is on screen first); Task 10 widget `another account sees no plan; quick exit and sign-out forget it` (mutants `plan_kept_across_accounts`, `plan_not_wiped`).

### Rulings (the spec wins over this plan; each records its cost if wrong)

- **Ruling R-37:** the chat's files live in `lib/features/dating/chat/` (§3.6 names `lib/features/dating/presentation/chat/`): W3–W5's Dating code has no `presentation/` layer, and `dating_boundaries_test.dart` checks imports by folder either way. Cost if wrong: a move.
- **Ruling R-38:** masking is computed once, at insert, by the SQL masker (`body_masked`); a read returns the body only to its sender or once both have swapped. A later masker version masks new messages only (history keeps the copy it was given). The TypeScript mirror has no runtime caller; it exists so the fixture proves both. Cost if wrong: a re-mask job.
- **Ruling R-39 (owner decision 1, DPIA):** the pair marker is `hmac('dating_pair_marker|' || least(ha, hb) || '|' || greatest(ha, hb), exclusion key)` over the pair's two phone HMACs: no names, no ids, no phone. It is written on Not for me, Block (card or chat), Unmatch and Close kindly, never on an expiry; without the key nothing is written and `dating.pair_marker.unwritten` is counted; `purge_after` is 2 years. Existing passes and blocks are backfilled. Cost if wrong: one table to drop.
- **Ruling R-40 (carry 5, DPIA):** a Dating-country change moves the identity answer to `held_identity_enc` (encrypted, as `identity_enc`) instead of erasing it, until the person answers again or their new country restricts; only the same-gender switch reads it, so a man–woman pair stays judged by what the person last said. Cost if wrong: one column.
- **Ruling R-41 (owner-visible):** 500 messages a day per sender (`check_dating_rate_limit('dating_message', 500, '1 day')`, W1's limiter): the spec sets no number. Cost: a busy legitimate sender reads "Too many tries. Try again later." after 500.
- **Ruling R-42:** a message push is the neutral `me_update` at most once per person per 15-minute bucket (`scheduled_notifications` purpose `chat:<bucket>`), only when the recipient's `chat_push` setting is not off; queueing never raises into the send. §3.13 says "while something is unread": the bucket is the collapse; the push itself names nothing.
- **Ruling R-43 (owner-visible):** Report (the Safety sheet, each card, the ended list) waits for W7a's report sheet, and "Become a couple on Attune" (after We met) for W9: neither row is shown before its wave. Cost: the spec's sheet is two rows short until then.
- **Ruling R-44:** the v1 lexicon is seeded as a `draft` row of `dating_safety_configs` whose `config_hash` must equal `sha256(config::text)`; an active row is frozen; the threat, sexual-pressure, harassment and identity sets are `[DRAFT]` until the cultural reviewer's Twi, Ga, Ewe and Pidgin entries. The dev script activates it under `DEV-` references.
- **Ruling R-45:** a guided answer is a `dating_messages` row (`kind = 'guided_answer'`, the question id) hidden from the other side until both have answered that question (`private.dating_message_visible`); it is masked like any message; a skip is for both (`dating_guided_skips`). Only the current question can be answered, once per side.
- **Ruling R-46 (owner-visible):** Check on me accepts a time from 5 minutes to 7 days ahead and replaces the person's last pending check-in; the plan's "When?" offers today to 7 days ahead. Share works offline; Check on me needs a connection. The check-in's "We met" opens Conversations, because the push carries no match.
- **Ruling R-47 (owner-visible):** the purge's windows not fixed by §4.8 are plan values: finished outbox rows after 7 days, rate-limit rows after 2 days, opener hashes after 30 days, session context after 30 days; safety events lose their people (not their counts) at 90 days.
- **Ruling R-48:** the dragnet's first messages (R-14, carry 6) are the person's sent messages in matches they were written in within the window; no input means no signal (pinned).
- **Ruling R-35 (carried):** the lock gate rebuilds every Dating page on an account change, so a screen-level D8 clear is a belt; two mutants that only remove that belt are equivalent and retired with their reason (Task 4's typed-words mutant; Task 10 removes both guards together).
- **Ruling R-36 (carried):** Block, Unmatch and Close kindly are gated by neither the phone nor eligibility, only membership.
- **Spec amendment (carry 3):** `edit_tests_T1.py` extends §6.1's platform list in the design spec (imo, botim, viber, wechat, discord, kik, youtube/yt, linkedin, reddit u/, momo, "the gram", line id) so the spec matches the masker.

Create `supabase/functions/_shared/dating_mask.test.ts` with exactly:

```typescript
// Spec 2 W6a (§6.1, §7.3 masking parity; carry 3): the TypeScript mirror
// gives every fixture case's expected output, and says a caption is dropped
// exactly for the caption_drop cases. dating_v2_chat_contracts.sql runs the
// same file through private.dating_mask_contacts and
// private.dating_caption_invites.
import { assertEquals } from "https://deno.land/std@0.224.0/assert/mod.ts";
import fixture from "../../tests/fixtures/dating_mask_cases.json" with { type: "json" };
import { captionInvites, MASK, MASK_VERSION, maskContacts } from "./dating_mask.ts";

Deno.test("the fixture is the version this mirror masks", () => {
  assertEquals(fixture.mask_version, MASK_VERSION);
  assertEquals(fixture.mask, MASK);
  if (fixture.cases.length < 207) throw new Error("the fixture lost cases");
  for (const scope of ["mask", "caption_drop"]) {
    if (!fixture.cases.some((c) => c.scope === scope)) throw new Error(`no ${scope} case`);
  }
});

Deno.test("every case masks to its expected output", () => {
  for (const c of fixture.cases) assertEquals(maskContacts(c.text), c.expect, `${c.note}: ${JSON.stringify(c.text)}`);
});

Deno.test("a caption is dropped exactly for the caption_drop cases", () => {
  for (const c of fixture.cases) {
    assertEquals(captionInvites(c.text), c.scope === "caption_drop", `${c.note}: ${JSON.stringify(c.text)}`);
  }
});

Deno.test("masking never lifts by itself: masked text masks to itself", () => {
  for (const c of fixture.cases) assertEquals(maskContacts(c.expect), c.expect, JSON.stringify(c.expect));
});

Deno.test("a 1,000-character message masks in bounded time", () => {
  const long = "Call me on 0244 123 456 or snap amab_gh, ama@x.io. ".repeat(20).slice(0, 1000);
  const t0 = performance.now();
  const out = maskContacts(long);
  if (performance.now() - t0 > 2000) throw new Error("too slow");
  if (out.includes("0244") || out.includes("ama@x.io")) throw new Error("a contact detail got through");
});
```

Create `supabase/functions/_shared/dating_mask.ts` with exactly (the SQL masker's line-by-line mirror: no runtime caller, Ruling R-38):

```typescript
// supabase/functions/_shared/dating_mask.ts
//
// Spec 2 W6a (§6.1 contact masking, §7.3 parity; carry 3): the TypeScript
// mirror of private.dating_mask_contacts(text) and
// private.dating_caption_invites(text) (migration
// 20261120100000_dating_v2_chat.sql). Both run the same fixture,
// supabase/tests/fixtures/dating_mask_cases.json, and must give the same
// output for every case. The SQL function is the one that masks: it fills
// dating_messages.body_masked at insert and masks photo captions on the
// card. Each span becomes "•••". Masking is presentation only: it never
// blocks delivery and never lifts by itself.
//
// How it works (the SQL function is a line-by-line port):
// 1. A shadow of the text, one entry per character it folds to, each
//    remembering the text character it came from: invisible characters
//    dropped, spaces to " ", NFKD, lower case, marks dropped, a few
//    look-alike letters folded (Cyrillic, Greek, West African).
// 2. Views of the shadow, one character for one: "li"/"ll" (leetspeak
//    next to a letter read as a letter, 1 as i or l), "dv" (dates and
//    times blanked, then o next to a digit as 0, l/i as 1).
// 3. Each rule's matches in its view are spans of the shadow; the spans are
//    merged and mapped back to the text, and each becomes "•••".

export const MASK = "•••";
export const MASK_VERSION = 1;

import { baseForm, matchForm } from "./unicode_normaliser.ts";

// The fold of one character. ASCII: lower case, any ASCII space a space.
// Anything else: Spec 1's own base form of that character (NFKC, lower case,
// invisible characters removed, look-alikes, other-script digits, its
// separators to a space; I-17: no fold table here), then its match form
// when that is still not ASCII (the West African fold, marks dropped). The
// character is read between two "0"s, which neither form changes into
// anything but one character, so a space or nothing survives as itself.
// A character outside the BMP that is left (an emoji) reads as U+FFFD.
function middle(t: string): string {
  const cs = [...t];
  return cs.slice(1, cs.length - 1).join("");
}

function foldChar(c: string): string {
  const cp = c.codePointAt(0)!;
  if (cp < 0x80) return /[\t\n\v\f\r ]/.test(c) ? " " : c.toLowerCase();
  let mid = middle(baseForm(`0${c}0`));
  if (/[^\u0000-\u007f]/.test(mid)) mid = middle(matchForm(`0${mid}0`));
  let out = "";
  for (const x of mid) out += x.codePointAt(0)! > 0xffff ? "\uFFFD" : x;
  return out;
}

type Shadow = { s: string; pos: number[]; changed: boolean[]; n: number };

function shadow(chars: string[]): Shadow {
  let s = "";
  const pos: number[] = [];
  const changed: boolean[] = [];
  chars.forEach((c, i) => {
    const f = foldChar(c);
    const plain = f === c.toLowerCase();
    for (const x of f) {
      s += x;
      pos.push(i);
      changed.push(!plain);
    }
  });
  return { s, pos, changed, n: chars.length };
}

// The views (each one character for one of the shadow).
const LEET: [string, string][] = [["4", "a"], ["3", "e"], ["0", "o"], ["5", "s"], ["7", "t"], ["@", "a"], ["\\$", "s"]];
function leet(s: string, one: string): string {
  let v = s;
  for (let pass = 0; pass < 2; pass++) {
    for (const [d, l] of [...LEET, ["1", one] as [string, string]]) {
      v = v.replace(new RegExp(`(?<=[a-z])${d}|${d}(?=[a-z])`, "g"), l);
    }
  }
  return v;
}

export const DATES = [
  "(?<![0-9])[0-9]{1,2}[/.-][0-9]{1,2}[/.-](?:[0-9]{4}|[0-9]{2})(?![0-9])",
  "(?<![0-9])[0-9]{4}[/.-][0-9]{1,2}[/.-][0-9]{1,2}(?![0-9])",
  "(?<![0-9])(?:19|20)[0-9]{2} ?[-/] ?(?:(?:19|20)[0-9]{2}|[0-9]{2})(?![0-9])",
  "(?<![0-9])[0-9]{1,2}:[0-5][0-9](?![0-9])",
];
function digitsView(s: string): string {
  let v = s;
  for (const d of DATES) v = v.replace(new RegExp(d, "g"), (m) => "x".repeat(m.length));
  for (let pass = 0; pass < 2; pass++) {
    v = v.replace(/(?<=[0-9])o|o(?=[0-9])/g, "0");
    v = v.replace(/(?<=[0-9])[li]|[li](?=[0-9])/g, "1");
  }
  return v;
}

// Word lists (alternations longest first, so both engines take the same).
const STRONG =
  "snap[ _-]?chat|tele[ _-]?gram|whats[ _-]?app|wats[ _-]?app|insta[ _-]?gram|face[ _-]?book|only[ _-]?fans|linked[ _-]?in|you[ _-]?tube|cash[ _-]?app|tik[ _-]?tok|pay[ _-]?pal|we[ _-]?chat|whtsapp|instgrm|twitter|discord|snpcht|wassap|reddit|tlgrm|botim|viber|venmo|whtsp|fcbk|momo|kik";
const WEAK = "the[ _-]?gram|messenger|threads|signal|insta|snap|line|imo|sc|ig|fb|tg|yt|wa|x";
const PROVIDER = "protonmail|googlemail|hotmail|outlook|icloud|proton|yahoo|gmail|ymail|aol|gmx";
const TLD = "info|link|app|biz|com|edu|gov|net|org|xyz|co|gh|io|ly|me|ng|uk";
const NUMWORD = "mmiensa|mmienu|nwotwe|triple|double|baako|seven|three|eight|nkron|hwee|enan|enum|four|five|nine|nsia|nson|zero|one|six|two|oh|o";
// Words that are never a handle ("my snapchat is ..." reads past "is").
export const STOP = new Set(
  "a about account again also am an and are at banned be blocked but call chat closed dead deleted different dm dms down for from gone handle here i id im in is it its later link locked me my name new no not now number of off ok okay old on or page please pls private public same so that the then there this to too up us user username video was we with yes you your".split(" "),
);
// Every platform word, for the evasive and spaced-out forms.
export const PLATFORM_WORDS = new Set(
  "snapchat telegram whatsapp watsapp instagram facebook onlyfans linkedin youtube cashapp tiktok paypal wechat whtsapp instgrm twitter discord snpcht wassap reddit tlgrm botim viber venmo whtsp fcbk momo kik thegram messenger threads signal insta snap line imo".split(" "),
);

// The platform words an evasive spelling is checked against.
const EVASIVE = [...PLATFORM_WORDS].sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length || (a < b ? -1 : 1)).join("|");

const W = "(?<![a-z0-9])";
export const E = "(?![a-z0-9])";
export const H = "(@?[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.-]*[a-z0-9_])";
export const GAP = `(?:[ :=@_-]+(?:is|its|id|handle|username|user|name|account|page)${E})*[ :=@_-]+`;

type Rule = { name: string; view: "s" | "li" | "ll" | "dv"; re: string; handle?: number; check?: string };

// The rules, in order. handle: the capture group holding a handle (a stop
// word there rejects the match). check: extra code below.
export const RULES: Rule[] = [
  // A platform that is only a platform, then a handle ("telegram amab").
  { name: "strong", view: "li", re: `${W}(?:${STRONG})${E}${GAP}${H}${E}`, handle: 1, check: "names" },
  { name: "strong", view: "ll", re: `${W}(?:${STRONG})${E}${GAP}${H}${E}`, handle: 1, check: "names" },
  // A platform word that is also a word, then ":", "@", "=" or "is".
  { name: "weak_said", view: "li", re: `${W}(?:${WEAK})${E}(?: *[:=@] *| +(?:is|id|handle|username) *[:=]? *)${H}${E}`, handle: 1 },
  { name: "weak_said", view: "ll", re: `${W}(?:${WEAK})${E}(?: *[:=@] *| +(?:is|id|handle|username) *[:=]? *)${H}${E}`, handle: 1 },
  // "my ig handle amab", "my sc is amab".
  { name: "my_platform", view: "li", re: `${W}(?:my|mine|our)(?: +|_)(?:${STRONG}|${WEAK})${E}(?:[ _]+(?:handle|id|name|username|account|page)${E})?(?:[ _]+is${E})?[ :=@_-]+${H}${E}`, handle: 1 },
  // A weak platform word, then something that looks like a handle.
  { name: "weak_handle", view: "li", re: `${W}(?:${WEAK})${E}[ :=_-]+(@?(?:[a-z0-9_.-]*[0-9_.][a-z0-9_.-]*[a-z0-9_]|[0-9]+))${E}`, handle: 1 },
  // A platform word spelled to dodge (sn@p, 5nap, ⓢⓝⓐⓟ), and its handle.
  { name: "evasive", view: "li", re: `${W}(${EVASIVE})${E}`, check: "evasive" },
  { name: "evasive", view: "ll", re: `${W}(${EVASIVE})${E}`, check: "evasive" },
  // Letters spaced out (s n a p c h a t, s.n.a.p) that spell a platform.
  { name: "spaced", view: "li", re: `${W}[a-z](?:[ ._-][a-z]){2,}${E}`, check: "spaced" },
  // "ama.b on IG", "amab on yt" (a whole message).
  { name: "on_platform", view: "li", re: `${W}(@?[a-z0-9_.-]*[0-9_.][a-z0-9_.-]*[a-z0-9_]) +on +(?:${STRONG}|${WEAK})${E}`, handle: 1 },
  { name: "on_platform_alone", view: "li", re: `^ *([a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.-]*[a-z0-9_]) +on +(?:${STRONG}|${WEAK}) *[.!]* *$`, handle: 1 },
  // A LINE id, a reddit u/handle, a "handle is ...".
  { name: "line_id", view: "li", re: `${W}line[ _-]?id${E}[ :=_-]*${H}${E}`, handle: 1 },
  { name: "reddit", view: "s", re: "(?<![a-z0-9/])u/[a-z0-9_-]+" },
  { name: "handle_is", view: "li", re: `${W}(?:handle|username|user[ _-]?name|user[ _-]?id)(?: +is)?[ :=@_-]+${H}${E}`, handle: 1 },
  // @handle (never "@ the beach").
  { name: "at_handle", view: "s", re: "(?<![a-z0-9_.@])@[a-z0-9_]+(?:\\.[a-z0-9_]+)*" },
  // Emails: symbol, then a provider in words.
  { name: "email", view: "s", re: "[a-z0-9._%+-]*[a-z0-9]@ ?[a-z0-9-]+(?:\\.[a-z0-9-]+)*" },
  { name: "email", view: "s", re: "[a-z0-9._%+-]*[a-z0-9] @[a-z0-9-]+(?:\\.[a-z0-9-]+)*" },
  { name: "email", view: "s", re: `${W}[a-z0-9._%+-]*[a-z0-9] +@ +(?:${PROVIDER})${E}(?:\\.[a-z]+)*` },
  { name: "provider", view: "s", re: `${W}([a-z0-9._-]+) +(?:at +|\\(at\\) *|\\[at\\] *)?(?:${PROVIDER})${E}(?: *(?:\\.|dot${E}|\\(dot\\)) *(?:com|net|co|uk)${E})*`, handle: 1 },
  { name: "provider", view: "s", re: `${W}(?:at +|\\(at\\) *|\\[at\\] *)?(?:${PROVIDER})${E}(?: *(?:\\.|dot${E}|\\(dot\\)) *(?:com|net|co|uk)${E})*` },
  // "name at domain dot tld" (and "(at)", "[at]", " . ").
  { name: "spelled_email", view: "s", re: `${W}[a-z0-9._-]+(?: dot [a-z0-9_-]+)* *(?: at |\\(at\\)|\\[at\\]) *[a-z0-9-]+(?: +[a-z0-9-]{2,})? *(?: dot |\\(dot\\)|\\[dot\\]| \\. ) *(?:${TLD})${E}(?:(?: dot |\\.)(?:${TLD})${E})*` },
  // "ama dot com".
  { name: "spelled_domain", view: "s", re: `${W}[a-z0-9-]+ (?:dot|\\.) (?:${TLD})${E}(?: (?:dot|\\.) (?:${TLD})${E})*` },
  // URLs and domains.
  { name: "url", view: "s", re: "https?://[^ ]+|(?<![a-z0-9])www\\.[^ ]+" },
  { name: "domain", view: "s", re: `${W}[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*(?:\\.[a-z0-9-]+)*\\.(?:${TLD})${E}(?:/[^ ]*)?` },
  { name: "domain_path", view: "s", re: `${W}[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*\\.[a-z]{2,}/[^ ]+` },
  // A cashtag; crypto addresses.
  { name: "cashtag", view: "s", re: "(?<![a-z0-9$])\\$[a-z][a-z0-9_]*" },
  { name: "crypto", view: "s", re: `${W}(?:0x[0-9a-f]{40}|bc1[0-9a-z]{25,62}|t[1-9a-z]{33})${E}` },
  // Phone numbers: 7 or more digits with spaces, dots, dashes, slashes or
  // brackets between, with or without +.
  { name: "phone", view: "dv", re: "(?<![0-9])\\+?\\(?[0-9](?:[ ._/()-]{0,3}[0-9]){6,}\\)?" },
  // Numbers in words (and digits among them), 7 or more in all.
  { name: "number_words", view: "s", re: `${W}(?:${NUMWORD}|[0-9]+)${E}(?:[ ,.-]+(?:${NUMWORD}|[0-9]+)${E})+`, check: "number_words" },
];

function compile(r: Rule): RegExp {
  return new RegExp(r.re, "g");
}
const COMPILED = RULES.map((r) => [r, compile(r)] as const);

function countNumber(m: string): number | null {
  let n = 0;
  let word = false;
  for (const t of m.split(/[ ,.-]+/)) {
    if (t === "") continue;
    if (/^[0-9]+$/.test(t)) n += t.length;
    else {
      n += 1;
      word = true;
    }
  }
  return word ? n : null;
}

type Span = [number, number];

// The shadow spans the rules find (half-open, in shadow positions).
function spans(sh: Shadow, chars: string[]): Span[] {
  const views: Record<Rule["view"], string> = { s: sh.s, li: leet(sh.s, "i"), ll: leet(sh.s, "l"), dv: digitsView(sh.s) };
  const out: Span[] = [];
  for (const [r, re] of COMPILED) {
    const v = views[r.view];
    let from = 0;
    while (from <= v.length) {
      re.lastIndex = from;
      const m = re.exec(v);
      if (!m) break;
      const a = m.index;
      let b = a + m[0].length;
      let ok = m[0].length > 0;
      if (ok && r.handle !== undefined) {
        const h = (m[r.handle] ?? "").replace(/^@/, "");
        if (STOP.has(h)) ok = false;
      }
      if (ok && r.check === "evasive") {
        ok = (sh.s.slice(a, b) !== v.slice(a, b) || sh.changed.slice(a, b).some((x) => x));
        if (ok) b = afterHandle(v, b);
      }
      if (ok && r.check === "spaced") {
        ok = PLATFORM_WORDS.has(m[0].replace(/[ ._-]/g, ""));
        if (ok) b = afterHandle(v, b);
      }
      if (ok && r.check === "number_words") ok = (countNumber(m[0]) ?? 0) >= 7;
      if (ok && r.check === "names") b = afterNames(v, b, chars, sh);
      if (ok) {
        out.push([a, b]);
        from = b;
      } else {
        from = a + 1;
      }
    }
  }
  return out;
}

// After an evasive or spaced platform word: its handle, if one follows.
function afterHandle(v: string, b: number): number {
  const m = new RegExp(`^${GAP}${H}${E}`).exec(v.slice(b));
  if (!m) return b;
  return STOP.has(m[1].replace(/^@/, "")) ? b : b + m[0].length;
}

// "facebook Ama Boateng": up to two more capitalised words of a name.
function afterNames(v: string, b: number, chars: string[], sh: Shadow): number {
  let end = b;
  for (let k = 0; k < 2; k++) {
    const m = /^ +([a-z][a-z'-]*[a-z])(?![a-z0-9])/.exec(v.slice(end));
    if (!m) break;
    const first = end + m[0].length - m[1].length;
    const c = chars[sh.pos[first]];
    if (c === undefined || c === c.toLowerCase() || STOP.has(m[1])) break;
    end += m[0].length;
  }
  return end;
}

/// The text with every contact detail span replaced by "•••".
export function maskContacts(text: string): string {
  const chars = [...text];
  const sh = shadow(chars);
  const found = spans(sh, chars)
    .filter(([a, b]) => b > a)
    .map(([a, b]) => [sh.pos[a], sh.pos[b - 1] + 1] as Span)
    .sort((x, y) => x[0] - y[0] || x[1] - y[1]);
  const merged: Span[] = [];
  for (const [a, b] of found) {
    const last = merged[merged.length - 1];
    if (last && a <= last[1]) last[1] = Math.max(last[1], b);
    else merged.push([a, b]);
  }
  let out = "";
  let i = 0;
  for (const [a, b] of merged) {
    out += chars.slice(i, a).join("") + MASK;
    i = b;
  }
  return out + chars.slice(i).join("");
}

export const INVITES = [
  `${W}(?:add|find|message|contact|hit|dm|text|call|ping|inbox|follow) +(?:me|us)${E}`,
  `${W}(?:slide|come) +into +my +dms?${E}`,
  `${W}(?:${STRONG}|${WEAK}) +(?:me|us)${E}`,
  `${W}of +link${E}`,
  `${W}link +in +(?:my +)?bio${E}`,
];
const INVITE_RES = INVITES.map((r) => new RegExp(r));

/// Captions only: an invitation to move off the app with nothing to mask
/// ("DM me", "add me on snap"). The card drops such a caption.
export function captionInvites(text: string): boolean {
  const sh = shadow([...text]);
  const li = leet(sh.s, "i");
  const ll = leet(sh.s, "l");
  return INVITE_RES.some((re) => re.test(li) || re.test(ll));
}
```

```sql
-- supabase/tests/dating_v2_chat_contracts.sql
-- Spec 2 W6a (§3.6, §4.3, §4.4, §4.5, §5.2 14, §6.1, §7.1, §7.3 masking
-- parity, §7.5 mutants 10-12; carries 1, 3, 5, 7): the masker gives every
-- fixture case's expected output and drops exactly the caption_drop
-- captions, failing closed (A); the card shows captions masked, never
-- dropped for a contact detail (B); the pair marker: written for "Not for
-- me" and a block, read by the single-pair predicate and by generation,
-- kept after leaving and after deleting an account, holding no id (C);
-- the kill switch keeps a man-woman pair whose other member lives
-- elsewhere (D); act names the match it made (E). Task 2 adds the chat
-- (F onward). Fixture ids are 6c6000..-nn and phones +1555960nn, unique
-- in the suite; each section takes its own range of nn (A-E 01-29).
-- One BEGIN ... ROLLBACK. Run only on attune_spec3a:
--   psql -X -q -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -d attune_spec3a -f supabase/tests/dating_v2_chat_contracts.sql
BEGIN;

\set cases `cat supabase/tests/fixtures/dating_mask_cases.json`
SELECT length(set_config('w6.cases', :'cases', true)) AS cases_loaded \gset

DO $$
BEGIN
  PERFORM set_config('app.settings.dating_exclusion_key', 'spec2-w6-chat-key', true);
  PERFORM set_config('app.settings.dating_field_key', 'spec2-w6-field-key-0123456789abcdef-0123', true);
  PERFORM set_config('app.settings.dating_push_key', 'spec2-w6-push-key', true);
END $$;
SELECT private.seed_dating_exclusion_key_fingerprint() AS seeded \gset
SELECT private.seed_dating_field_key_fingerprint() AS seeded_field \gset
INSERT INTO public.feature_flags (key, enabled)
VALUES ('dating_mode_enabled', true), ('dating_candidate_generation', true), ('dating_same_gender_matching', true),
       ('dating_match_messaging', true), ('dating_profile_photos', true)
ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET enabled = true;
SELECT public.publish_country_config('GH', '{"dating_enabled": true, "open_regions": ["AA", "AH"]}') AS gh \gset
SELECT public.publish_country_config('GB', '{"dating_enabled": true}') AS gb \gset
UPDATE public.dating_algorithm_configs SET state = 'retired', retired_at = now() WHERE state = 'active';
UPDATE public.dating_algorithm_configs
   SET state = 'active', activated_at = now(), product_review_ref = 'DEV-product', clinical_review_ref = 'DEV-clinical',
       fairness_review_ref = 'DEV-fairness', safety_review_ref = 'DEV-safety'
 WHERE version = 'introductions_v2';

CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.id(p_n text) RETURNS uuid LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE AS $$
  SELECT ('6c600000-0000-0000-0000-0000000000' || p_n)::uuid;
$$;
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.dev(p_n text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE AS $$
  SELECT md5('w6-device-' || p_n);
$$;
-- A current candidate (the matching contract's fixture person) with every
-- answer in [o] over the defaults; "phone" names another fixture's number
-- (the same person on a new account).
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.ready(p_n text, o jsonb DEFAULT '{}') RETURNS uuid LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE
  v_u constant uuid := pg_temp.id(p_n);
  d constant jsonb := '{"identity": "woman", "seeking": ["man"], "country": "GH", "region": "AA", "years": 30,
    "days": 40, "phone": null, "name": null}'::jsonb || o;
  v_phone constant text := '+1555960' || coalesce(d ->> 'phone', p_n);
BEGIN
  INSERT INTO auth.users (id, phone, phone_confirmed_at, created_at)
  VALUES (v_u, ltrim(v_phone, '+'), now(), now() - make_interval(days => (d ->> 'days')::int));
  INSERT INTO public.users (id, phone, display_name, path, created_at, eula_accepted_at, eula_version)
  VALUES (v_u, v_phone, 'Chat ' || p_n, 'dating', now() - make_interval(days => (d ->> 'days')::int), now(), '2');
  INSERT INTO public.account_birthdates (user_id, birth_date)
  VALUES (v_u, (current_date - make_interval(years => (d ->> 'years')::int) - interval '10 days')::date);
  INSERT INTO public.dating_enrollments (user_id, state, opted_in_at, activated_at) VALUES (v_u, 'active', now(), now());
  INSERT INTO public.dating_profiles (user_id, display_name, profile_state, moderation_state, verification_state,
    country_code, region_code, intention, timeline_visible, not_married_attested_at, attestation_version,
    children_have, children_want, faith_visible)
  VALUES (v_u, coalesce(d ->> 'name', 'Chat ' || p_n), 'active', 'approved', 'verified', d ->> 'country', d ->> 'region',
    'committed', true, now(), '2026-10-v1', false, 'yes', false);
  INSERT INTO public.dating_preferences (user_id, min_age, max_age, region_scope, open_abroad, want_must_match, exclude_parents)
  VALUES (v_u, 25, 40, 'region', '{}', false, false);
  INSERT INTO public.dating_profile_sensitive (user_id, identity_enc, seeking_enc)
  VALUES (v_u, private.dating_field_encrypt(jsonb_build_object('identity', d ->> 'identity', 'pools', jsonb_build_array(d ->> 'identity'))),
          private.dating_field_encrypt(jsonb_build_object('seeking', d -> 'seeking')));
  INSERT INTO public.dating_prompts (user_id, position, prompt_id, answer, moderation_state)
  SELECT v_u, n, CASE n WHEN 1 THEN 'perfect_sunday' ELSE 'ask_me_about' END, 'A chat fixture answer ' || n, 'approved'
    FROM generate_series(1, 2) AS n;
  INSERT INTO public.dating_profile_photos (user_id, storage_key, position, moderation_state, csam_state)
  SELECT v_u, 'w6/' || v_u || '/' || n || '.jpg', n, 'approved', 'clear' FROM generate_series(1, 2) AS n;
  INSERT INTO public.dating_age_verifications (user_id, age_method, age_result, age_vendor, age_decided_at,
    identity_method, identity_result, identity_vendor, identity_decided_at)
  VALUES (v_u, 'id', 'pass', 'contract', now(), 'liveness_compare', 'pass', 'contract', now());
  INSERT INTO public.dating_consent_events (user_id, idempotency_key, purpose, action, policy_version, occurred_at)
  SELECT v_u, 'w6-consent-' || p, p, 'granted', 'dating-2.0', clock_timestamp()
    FROM unnest(ARRAY['dating_terms', 'dating_opt_in', 'special_category_profile']) AS p;
  INSERT INTO public.dating_trusted_devices (user_id, device_hash, verified_at, last_seen_at)
  VALUES (v_u, encode(extensions.digest(pg_temp.dev(p_n), 'sha256'), 'hex'), now(), now());
  RETURN v_u;
END $$;
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.as_person(p uuid, p_device text) RETURNS void LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
BEGIN
  EXECUTE 'SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated';
  PERFORM set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', p::text, true);
  PERFORM set_config('request.jwt.claims', json_build_object('sub', p, 'role', 'authenticated')::text, true);
  PERFORM set_config('request.headers', CASE WHEN p_device IS NULL THEN '' ELSE json_build_object('x-attune-device', p_device)::text END, true);
END $$;
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.as_db() RETURNS void LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
BEGIN
  RESET ROLE;
  PERFORM set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', '', true);
  PERFORM set_config('request.jwt.claims', '', true);
  PERFORM set_config('request.headers', '', true);
END $$;
-- [p_sql] as [p] on the phone [p_device] (NULL: no header); the error's
-- SQLSTATE and message when it raises.
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.call(p uuid, p_device text, p_sql text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE
  v text;
BEGIN
  PERFORM pg_temp.as_person(p, p_device);
  EXECUTE p_sql INTO v;
  PERFORM pg_temp.as_db();
  RETURN coalesce(v, '<NULL>');
EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
  PERFORM pg_temp.as_db();
  RETURN SQLSTATE || ':' || SQLERRM;
END $$;
-- The same, as jsonb (a raised error is {"raised": "<state>:<message>"}).
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.j(p uuid, p_n text, p_sql text) RETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE
  v text := pg_temp.call(p, pg_temp.dev(p_n), p_sql);
BEGIN
  RETURN v::jsonb;
EXCEPTION WHEN invalid_text_representation THEN
  RETURN jsonb_build_object('raised', v);
END $$;
-- Runs [p_setup] and [p_probe], then undoes both (a savepoint).
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.probe(p_setup text[], p_probe text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$
DECLARE
  v text;
  v_sql text;
BEGIN
  BEGIN
    FOREACH v_sql IN ARRAY p_setup LOOP
      EXECUTE v_sql;
    END LOOP;
    EXECUTE p_probe INTO v;
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'w6:probe_undo';
  EXCEPTION WHEN raise_exception THEN
    IF SQLERRM IS DISTINCT FROM 'w6:probe_undo' THEN
      RAISE;
    END IF;
  END;
  RETURN coalesce(v, '<NULL>');
END $$;
-- Both orders of the single-pair predicate, as 'tt', 'ff' or a mix.
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.hf(p_a uuid, p_b uuid) RETURNS text LANGUAGE sql AS $$
  SELECT left(public.dating_pair_passes_hard_filters(p_a, p_b)::text, 1) || left(public.dating_pair_passes_hard_filters(p_b, p_a)::text, 1);
$$;
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.metric(p_key text) RETURNS bigint LANGUAGE sql AS $$
  SELECT coalesce((SELECT sum(c.count) FROM public.metric_counters AS c
                    WHERE c.day = (now() AT TIME ZONE 'utc')::date AND c.key = p_key), 0)::bigint;
$$;
-- An introduction both see now (presented), and its id.
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.intro(p_a uuid, p_b uuid) RETURNS uuid LANGUAGE sql AS $$
  INSERT INTO public.dating_introductions (pair_key, user_low_id, user_high_id, state, algorithm_version,
                                           visible_from_low, visible_from_high, expires_at)
  VALUES (public.dating_pair_key(p_a, p_b), least(p_a, p_b), greatest(p_a, p_b), 'presented', 'introductions_v2',
          now() - interval '1 hour', now() - interval '1 hour', now() + interval '6 days')
  RETURNING id;
$$;
-- The pair's marker exists.
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.marked(p_a uuid, p_b uuid) RETURNS boolean LANGUAGE sql AS $$
  SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.dating_pair_markers AS k
                  WHERE k.pair_code = private.dating_pair_code(public.dating_phone_hmac_for_user(p_a),
                                                               public.dating_phone_hmac_for_user(p_b)));
$$;
CREATE FUNCTION pg_temp.key(p text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE sql AS $$
  SELECT 'w6-' || p || '-' || replace(gen_random_uuid()::text, '-', '');
$$;

-- =====================================================================
-- A. (§6.1, §7.3; carry 3) The masker: every fixture case masks to its
--    expected output and drops a caption exactly for the caption_drop
--    cases (the TypeScript mirror runs the same file); masked text masks
--    to itself (masking never lifts by itself); the wrapper the insert
--    uses fails closed when the masker raises; the masker is IMMUTABLE.
-- =====================================================================
DO $$
DECLARE
  v_cases constant jsonb := current_setting('w6.cases')::jsonb;
  v_bad text[] := '{}';
  c jsonb;
BEGIN
  IF (v_cases ->> 'mask_version')::int IS DISTINCT FROM 1 OR jsonb_array_length(v_cases -> 'cases') < 207 THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the masking fixture is not version 1 with its 207 cases';
  END IF;
  FOR c IN SELECT x FROM jsonb_array_elements(v_cases -> 'cases') AS x LOOP
    IF private.dating_mask_contacts(c ->> 'text') IS DISTINCT FROM c ->> 'expect' THEN
      v_bad := v_bad || format('%s -> %s (want %s)', c ->> 'text', private.dating_mask_contacts(c ->> 'text'), c ->> 'expect');
    END IF;
    IF private.dating_caption_invites(c ->> 'text') IS DISTINCT FROM (c ->> 'scope' = 'caption_drop') THEN
      v_bad := v_bad || format('%s: caption %s', c ->> 'text', CASE WHEN c ->> 'scope' = 'caption_drop' THEN 'kept' ELSE 'dropped' END);
    END IF;
    IF private.dating_mask_contacts(c ->> 'expect') IS DISTINCT FROM c ->> 'expect' THEN
      v_bad := v_bad || format('%s: masks again', c ->> 'expect');
    END IF;
  END LOOP;
  IF cardinality(v_bad) > 0 THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the masker differs from the fixture: %', v_bad;
  END IF;
  IF (SELECT p.provolatile FROM pg_proc AS p WHERE p.oid = 'private.dating_mask_contacts(text)'::regprocedure) <> 'i' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: dating_mask_contacts is not IMMUTABLE';
  END IF;
  IF pg_temp.probe(ARRAY['CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION private.dating_mask_contacts(p_text text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $f$ BEGIN RAISE EXCEPTION ''w6:boom''; END $f$'],
       'SELECT private.dating_mask_contacts_safe(''call 0244 123 456 or ama@x.io or www.x.gh now'')')
     IS DISTINCT FROM 'call ••• ••• ••• or ••• or ••• now' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: a masker that raises does not fail closed: %',
      pg_temp.probe(ARRAY['CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION private.dating_mask_contacts(p_text text) RETURNS text LANGUAGE plpgsql IMMUTABLE AS $f$ BEGIN RAISE EXCEPTION ''w6:boom''; END $f$'],
       'SELECT private.dating_mask_contacts_safe(''call 0244 123 456 or ama@x.io or www.x.gh now'')');
  END IF;
END $$;

-- =====================================================================
-- B. (§3.4, §6.1; carry 3, owner 2026-11-20) Captions on the card: a
--    contact detail is masked and the caption shown; an invitation off
--    the app or an unkind caption stays off; a caption W5b's helper
--    dropped for nothing (one of the 15 documented false positives) now
--    shows; the helper is gone; a caption edit masks again.
-- =====================================================================
DO $$
DECLARE
  a constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('01');
  b constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('02', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  v_i uuid;
  v_card jsonb;
  v_caps text[];
BEGIN
  UPDATE public.dating_profile_photos SET caption = 'Lunch at Labadi, call 0244 123 456' WHERE user_id = b AND position = 1;
  UPDATE public.dating_profile_photos SET caption = 'DM me' WHERE user_id = b AND position = 2;
  INSERT INTO public.dating_profile_photos (user_id, storage_key, position, moderation_state, csam_state, caption)
  VALUES (b, 'w6/' || b || '/3.jpg', 3, 'approved', 'clear', 'Snap peas from the garden');
  v_i := pg_temp.intro(a, b);
  v_card := private.dating_card(a, b, (SELECT i FROM public.dating_introductions AS i WHERE i.id = v_i));
  v_caps := ARRAY(SELECT coalesce(x ->> 'caption', '<none>') FROM jsonb_array_elements(v_card -> 'photos') AS x ORDER BY (x ->> 'position')::int);
  IF v_caps IS DISTINCT FROM ARRAY['Lunch at Labadi, call •••', '<none>', 'Snap peas from the garden'] THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the card''s captions are % (expected the number masked, the invitation off, the peas shown)', v_caps;
  END IF;
  UPDATE public.dating_profile_photos SET caption = 'Find me at amab.com' WHERE user_id = b AND position = 3;
  IF (SELECT f.caption_masked FROM public.dating_profile_photos AS f WHERE f.user_id = b AND f.position = 3)
     IS DISTINCT FROM 'Find me at •••' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: an edited caption is not masked again';
  END IF;
  IF to_regprocedure('private.dating_caption_has_contact(text)') IS NOT NULL THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: W5b''s caption helper is still there';
  END IF;
END $$;

-- =====================================================================
-- C. (§5.2 14, §3.6, §4.8; owner decision 2026-11-20, carry 1) The pair
--    marker. "Not for me" and a block keep it; the single-pair predicate
--    and generation read it; it outlives leaving Dating (the introduction
--    deleted) and deleting an account (the same number on a new account
--    is still never introduced); it holds a code and dates only, purged
--    at 2 years; without the key nothing is kept and it is counted.
-- =====================================================================
DO $$
DECLARE
  v_at constant timestamptz := date_trunc('day', now() AT TIME ZONE 'UTC') AT TIME ZONE 'UTC' + interval '3 hours 17 minutes';
  a constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('03');
  b constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('04', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  c constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('05');
  e constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('06', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  v_i uuid;
  v jsonb;
  v_n bigint;
  b2 uuid;
BEGIN
  -- C1. "Not for me": the marker, and the predicate refuses the pair even
  --     with the introduction gone (as after one of them leaves).
  v_i := pg_temp.intro(a, b);
  v := pg_temp.j(a, '03', format('SELECT public.act_on_dating_introduction(%L, %L, ''passed'')', pg_temp.key('pass'), v_i));
  IF v ->> 'ok' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true' OR NOT pg_temp.marked(a, b) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: "Not for me" kept no pair marker (%)', v;
  END IF;
  DELETE FROM public.dating_introductions WHERE id = v_i;
  IF pg_temp.hf(a, b) IS DISTINCT FROM 'ff' OR pg_temp.hf(c, e) IS DISTINCT FROM 'tt' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the predicate ignores the marker (passed pair %, control %)', pg_temp.hf(a, b), pg_temp.hf(c, e);
  END IF;
  -- C2. Generation: the marked pair is never introduced; the control is.
  UPDATE public.dating_enrollments SET state = 'paused', pause_reason = 'user' WHERE state = 'active' AND user_id NOT IN (a, b);
  v := public.run_dating_generation_tick(v_at, 'GH');
  IF (v #>> '{runs,0,created}')::int IS DISTINCT FROM 0
     OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.dating_introductions WHERE pair_key = public.dating_pair_key(a, b)) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: generation introduced a marked pair again (%)', v;
  END IF;
  IF pg_temp.probe(ARRAY['DELETE FROM public.dating_pair_markers', 'DELETE FROM public.dating_generation_runs'],
       format('SELECT public.run_dating_generation_tick(%L, ''GH'') #>> ''{runs,0,created}''', v_at)) IS DISTINCT FROM '1' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: without the marker the control run did not introduce the pair';
  END IF;
  UPDATE public.dating_enrollments SET state = 'active', pause_reason = NULL WHERE user_id IN (c, e);
  -- C3. A deleted account: the same number on a new account is still
  --     never introduced to a.
  DELETE FROM public.dating_trusted_devices WHERE user_id = b;
  DELETE FROM auth.users WHERE id = b;
  DELETE FROM public.users WHERE id = b;
  b2 := pg_temp.ready('07', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"], "phone": "04"}');
  IF pg_temp.hf(a, b2) IS DISTINCT FROM 'ff' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the marker did not survive the account''s deletion (%)', pg_temp.hf(a, b2);
  END IF;
  -- C4. A block (through the card) keeps it too.
  v_i := pg_temp.intro(c, e);
  v := pg_temp.j(c, '05', format('SELECT public.block_dating_introduction(%L, %L)', pg_temp.key('block'), v_i));
  IF v ->> 'ok' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true' OR NOT pg_temp.marked(c, e) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: a block kept no pair marker (%)', v;
  END IF;
  -- C5. A code and its dates only; kept 2 years.
  IF (SELECT array_agg(column_name::text ORDER BY ordinal_position) FROM information_schema.columns
       WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_name = 'dating_pair_markers') IS DISTINCT FROM ARRAY['pair_code', 'created_at', 'purge_after']
     OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.dating_pair_markers WHERE purge_after <> created_at + interval '2 years') THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the marker holds more than a code and its dates, or is not kept 2 years';
  END IF;
  -- C6. No key: nothing kept, counted, and the pass still answers.
  v_n := pg_temp.metric('dating.pair_marker.unwritten');
  IF pg_temp.probe(ARRAY['SELECT set_config(''app.settings.dating_exclusion_key'', '''', true)'],
       format('SELECT private.dating_mark_pair(%L, %L)::text || '':'' || pg_temp.metric(''dating.pair_marker.unwritten'')', a, c))
     IS DISTINCT FROM 'false:' || (v_n + 1) THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: a marker without the key was not refused and counted';
  END IF;
END $$;

-- =====================================================================
-- D. (§2.4; carry 5) A man who moves from Ghana to the UK is asked his
--    gender again; until he answers, the identity he held is kept
--    (encrypted) for the kill switch alone, so a Ghana switch keeps his
--    man-woman match open. A pair with no identity at all still closes
--    (fail-safe), as does a same-gender one; his answer clears what was
--    held; nothing is held in a switched country.
-- =====================================================================
DO $$
DECLARE
  w constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('10');
  m constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('11', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  x constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('12');
  y constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('13', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  w2 constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('16', '{"seeking": ["woman"]}');
  w3 constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('17', '{"country": "GB", "region": "TLI", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  v jsonb;
BEGIN
  INSERT INTO public.dating_matches (user_low_id, user_high_id, state)
  VALUES (least(w, m), greatest(w, m), 'active'), (least(x, y), greatest(x, y), 'active'),
         (least(w2, w3), greatest(w2, w3), 'active');
  v := pg_temp.j(m, '11', format('SELECT public.save_dating_location(%L, ''GB'', ''TLI'', NULL, ''region'')', pg_temp.key('move')));
  IF v ->> 'ok' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'
     OR (SELECT s.identity_enc IS NULL AND s.held_identity_enc IS NOT NULL FROM public.dating_profile_sensitive AS s WHERE s.user_id = m)
        IS NOT TRUE THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: a move did not ask again while holding the identity (%)', v;
  END IF;
  UPDATE public.dating_profile_sensitive SET identity_enc = NULL WHERE user_id = y; -- no identity, nothing held
  PERFORM public.apply_same_gender_switch('GH');
  IF (SELECT state FROM public.dating_matches WHERE pair_key = public.dating_pair_key(w, m)) IS DISTINCT FROM 'active' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the switch closed a man-woman match because the man moved and has not answered again (carry 5)';
  END IF;
  IF (SELECT state || '/' || closed_reason FROM public.dating_matches WHERE pair_key = public.dating_pair_key(x, y))
     IS DISTINCT FROM 'closed/other'
     OR (SELECT state || '/' || closed_reason FROM public.dating_matches WHERE pair_key = public.dating_pair_key(w2, w3))
     IS DISTINCT FROM 'closed/other' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: the switch left open a pair with no identity, or a same-gender pair';
  END IF;
  v := pg_temp.j(m, '11', format('SELECT public.save_dating_identity(%L, ''man'', NULL, NULL, NULL, true)', pg_temp.key('d5')));
  IF v ->> 'ok' IS DISTINCT FROM 'true'
     OR (SELECT s.held_identity_enc FROM public.dating_profile_sensitive AS s WHERE s.user_id = m) IS NOT NULL THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: answering again did not clear the held identity (%)', v;
  END IF;
  IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.dating_profile_sensitive AS s JOIN public.dating_profiles AS p ON p.user_id = s.user_id
              WHERE p.country_code = 'GH' AND s.held_identity_enc IS NOT NULL AND s.user_id::text LIKE '6c60%') THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: an identity is held in a switched country';
  END IF;
END $$;

-- =====================================================================
-- E. (§3.5; carry 7) Act names the match its answer made, the same on a
--    replay; a pass names none.
-- =====================================================================
DO $$
DECLARE
  a constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('14');
  b constant uuid := pg_temp.ready('15', '{"identity": "man", "seeking": ["woman"]}');
  v_i constant uuid := pg_temp.intro(a, b);
  k constant text := pg_temp.key('int');
  v1 jsonb;
  v2 jsonb;
  v3 jsonb;
BEGIN
  v1 := pg_temp.j(a, '14', format('SELECT public.act_on_dating_introduction(%L, %L, ''interested'')', pg_temp.key('int'), v_i));
  v2 := pg_temp.j(b, '15', format('SELECT public.act_on_dating_introduction(%L, %L, ''interested'')', k, v_i));
  v3 := pg_temp.j(b, '15', format('SELECT public.act_on_dating_introduction(%L, %L, ''interested'')', k, v_i));
  IF v1 ? 'match_id' AND v1 ->> 'match_id' IS NOT NULL
     OR v2 ->> 'match_id' IS DISTINCT FROM (SELECT id::text FROM public.dating_matches WHERE introduction_id = v_i)
     OR v3 ->> 'match_id' IS DISTINCT FROM v2 ->> 'match_id' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'CONTRACT VIOLATED: act does not name the match it made (first %, second %, replay %)', v1, v2, v3;
  END IF;
END $$;
```
